  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F33.3, R43.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s my eating, I think. It’s gotten kind of turned around on me lately. Some days I’ll realize it’s late in the afternoon and I never ate lunch at all, like it just slipped right out of my head. And then other days I’m standing in the kitchen at the counter, picking at crackers or sweets, not even making a proper meal, just eating them one after another.\n\nThe strange part is it’s not even like I’m enjoying it much. Things don’t taste right to me, or they taste flat, but I still keep nibbling. It feels unlike me, and I guess it worried me enough that I thought I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that foods didn’t taste right?\n\nPatient: I can’t put an exact day on it, honestly. I think I first started noticing it around the same time my eating got so mixed up. I’d be halfway through the day and realize I’d skipped lunch completely, not on purpose, just… it never occurred to me. And then other times I’d be in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets right there at the counter, and I’d think, “Why am I still eating this? It doesn’t even taste good.”\n\nIt was that flat taste that caught my attention. Like the food was there, but it wasn’t really registering the way it should. Not spoiled or anything, just not right. And that’s part of what made me come in, because it felt so unlike my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything obvious like that, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the day has already lost its color before I’ve even gotten dressed. I’ll just lie there for a bit and feel like everything is sort of dimmed down, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s a hard feeling to describe because it’s not one big dramatic thing. It’s more like I open my eyes and there’s this weight sitting there with me, and I think, “Oh… it’s going to be that kind of day again.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel most unlike myself. I used to have my little things in the week, you know? My watercolor class, my quilting circle, book club… I would get my bag ready by the door with whatever I needed, and I’d sort of look forward to getting out and seeing everyone.\n\nLately, though, I’ll still put the bag there sometimes, almost out of habit, and then I just… leave it sitting there. I’ll look at it and think, “I should go,” but there’s no pull to it. It’s not even that I hate the idea exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go. And then the time passes, and I feel bad about it afterward, but not enough to change it the next time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping differently than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed some big clear change like staying up all night or sleeping the whole day away. It’s more that I haven’t been paying close attention to it, honestly.\n\nSome nights feel normal enough, and other nights I’m just sort of aware of lying there and thinking, “Am I sleeping right?” but I couldn’t tell you exactly if it’s really different from usual or if I’m just noticing everything more because I haven’t been feeling quite myself. So… I don’t know. Nothing that stands out to me the way the other things have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more forgetful or having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s been happening with little ordinary things, which is what bothers me. Not big complicated tasks, just simple things around the house.\n\nI’ll sit at the kitchen table and think, “All right, I need to call the pharmacy… and I should open the mail too,” and then I just sort of stare at both of those ideas like they’re too much to sort through. My mind gets foggy, like there’s cotton in it, and I can’t decide what to do first. Then ten minutes go by, or longer, and I haven’t called anyone and I haven’t opened the mail either. I’m just sitting there feeling foolish because it shouldn’t be that hard.\n\nIt’s not every single second, but enough that I’ve noticed it. Simple decisions feel harder than they ought to.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: I’d say… about a year and two months now, give or take. It’s hard to mark the exact day something like that starts, because it wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything had changed. It was more like the color slowly drained out of things, little by little, and then at some point I realized, “Oh… this has been going on for quite a while.”\n\nBut yes, if I’m being as honest and accurate as I can, it’s been most days for about fourteen months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think hopeless is probably the right word, though I don’t always call it that to myself.\n\nMost mornings, when I first wake up, there’s this heavy, low feeling already sitting there, before I’ve even gotten dressed or done anything. It’s like the day has lost its color before it’s even started. I’ll lie there for a bit and think, “Well… here we go again,” not in a dramatic way, just tired inside, like I can’t quite imagine the day turning into anything better.\n\nIt isn’t that I’m crying every morning or saying anything out loud. It’s more quiet than that. Just this weight, this dullness, like everything ahead of me is already gray. So yes… lately, I have been feeling pretty hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually up or energetic for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I get a little more done, or I feel a bit less weighed down for a few hours, but not what I would call unusually up or full of energy for several days in a row.\n\nAt my age, energy sort of comes and goes for ordinary reasons, you know? If I sleep a little better one night, I might tidy the kitchen or answer a few calls, and then I think, “Oh, maybe today won’t be so bad.” But it doesn’t turn into several days of feeling revved up or anything like that. I can’t think of a stretch where I was unusually energetic in a way that stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I have had thoughts like that, though it’s hard to say out loud because I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself. I’m not sitting there working out how I would do it or anything like that.\n\nBut I do catch myself thinking… if I didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be all right. Sometimes it even feels like it might be a relief, not just for me, but for everyone around me. Like I’m tired, and maybe other people are tired of me being this way too.\n\nI know that sounds awful. I don’t mean that I’m planning anything. It’s more like a quiet thought that comes when I’m very low, that maybe just not being here would be easier.\n\nDoctor: During this past year or so, was there ever a stretch of more than two months when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there was a stretch that long. I’ve had little times where things felt a bit quieter, like the worries about the records weren’t pressing on me quite as much, or I could get through a few days without feeling so stirred up about that. But when I really look back over the past year, the sadness has still been there most of the time.\n\nIt started taking over more after my sister became ill, and since then it’s like… even if one worry eased up for a while, I still didn’t feel like myself. I wasn’t really enjoying things, and there was this dullness underneath everything. So, no, I can’t honestly say there were more than two months where I wasn’t depressed. It’s been more like it faded a little here and there, but never really lifted for that long.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year and two months, were you feeling depressed most days for at least another ten months?\n\nPatient: No… not another ten months before that. I don’t think so.\n\nWhen I try to put a shape to it, the really low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months. Before that, I may have had ordinary sad days, or times I felt tired or discouraged, but not depressed most days for that long. It’s this past year or so that feels like it settled in and stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have been worrying a lot. It’s not just ordinary worrying, like about bills or getting to appointments, though there is some of that too. It’s more this feeling that I can’t put down, about the family papers and records.\n\nI know it sounds odd when I say it out loud, and I’m aware people may think I’m making too much of it, but I keep feeling like certain dates and names in the family papers are being changed, or hidden from me somehow. Like I’ll look at something and think, “That wasn’t how it was before,” or I’ll feel like a name has been placed there or left out for a reason. And then my mind starts going over and over it.\n\nSometimes it feels like the hospital, or maybe someone in the family, is sending little messages through the records. Not a message spoken out loud, exactly, but like the way the dates line up, or which names are included, is meant to tell me something. I know that probably sounds strange, but it feels very real when I’m in it, and then I can’t stop thinking about what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that the family papers and records are being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say “for certain” in the way you mean… like if you asked me to prove it, I don’t have proof I could lay out on the table. But it feels very strong to me, stronger than just a passing thought.\n\nI keep feeling that certain dates and names in the family papers have been changed, or maybe kept from me, and then I start wondering why that would be. It’s like there’s a pattern there that I’m supposed to notice. Sometimes I think it’s the hospital, or someone in the family, using the records to send me little messages—not in words exactly, but in what’s included, what’s missing, how things line up.\n\nI know it sounds odd. I do know that. Part of me can hear how it must sound to someone else. But when I’m looking at those papers, it doesn’t feel odd at all—it feels like something is really happening, and I can’t just dismiss it.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about the family papers being changed or used to send you messages ever been present during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… there was a time like that. It’s a little hard to explain, because the low mood sort of comes and goes in heaviness, but there were several weeks when I wasn’t feeling as sunk down as usual.\n\nI was eating more normally then, and I was getting dressed more normally too—not just sitting around in the same things or feeling like everything was too much effort. So in that sense, my mood had eased some.\n\nBut even during those weeks, I still had that strong feeling about the papers. I still felt like the dates and names in the records were being changed, or arranged in a certain way, like they were meant to signal something to me. It didn’t go away just because I was functioning a bit better. If anything, I remember being more able to look at the papers and think about them, and the feeling was still there, that something in them was being altered or used to tell me something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way people usually mean, like hearing a clear voice in the room when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a sound from another room, and then when I stop and listen there isn’t anything. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIf you mean like actual words, like someone speaking when no one is there, then no… I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Yes… I think they do, or at least eating feels different than it used to. It’s hard for me to separate “taste” from just not wanting food, if that makes sense.

Some things don’t taste right anymore. Not spoiled exactly, but flat, or strange, or just not like I remember. And then my eating gets all mixed up around that. Some days I’ll realize I’ve forgotten lunch completely, like the whole middle of the day went by and I never even thought to eat. It’s like the day has lost its color before it’s even started. I’ll lie there for a bit and think, “Well… here we go again,” not in a dramatic way, just tired inside, like I can’t quite imagine the day turning into anything better.\n\nIt isn’t that I’m crying every morning or saying anything out loud. I know that probably sounds strange, but it feels very real when I’m in it, and then I can’t stop thinking about what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that the family papers and records are being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say “for certain” in the way you mean… like if you asked me to prove it, I don’t have proof I could lay out on the table. I still felt like the dates and names in the records were being changed, or arranged in a certain way, like they were meant to signal something to me. If anything, I remember being more able to look at the papers and think about them, and the feeling was still there, that something in them was being altered or used to tell me something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way people usually mean, like hearing a clear voice in the room when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a sound from another room, and then when I stop and listen there isn’t anything. But I don’t know if that’s just the house settling, or my ears, or me being tired. I don’t really have a voice talking to me, telling me things, or carrying on a conversation. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIf you mean like actual words, like someone speaking when no one is there, then no… I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do foods actually taste different to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do, or at least eating feels different than it used to. I’ll look at it and think, “I should go,” but there’s no pull to it. It’s not even that I hate the idea exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go. It’s like the day has lost its color before it’s even started. I’ll lie there for a bit and think, “Well… here we go again,” not in a dramatic way, just tired inside, like I can’t quite imagine the day turning into anything better.\n\nIt isn’t that I’m crying every morning or saying anything out loud. It’s more quiet than that. Just this weight, this dullness, like everything ahead of me is already gray. So yes… lately, I have been feeling pretty hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually up or energetic for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I get a little more done, or I feel a bit less weighed down for a few hours, but not what I would call unusually up or full of energy for several days in a row.\n\nAt my age, energy sort of comes and goes for ordinary reasons, you know? If I sleep a little better one night, I might tidy the kitchen or answer a few calls, and then I think, “Oh, maybe today won’t be so bad.” But it doesn’t turn into several days of feeling revved up or anything like that. I can’t think of a stretch where I was unusually energetic in a way that stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I have had thoughts like that, though it’s hard to say out loud because I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself. I’m not sitting there working out how I would do it or anything like that.\n\nBut I do catch myself thinking… if I didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be all right. It’s gotten turned around.\n\n[Last Question]\nFor at least one month, have you had that belief about the family papers and records being changed or used to send you messages?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Psychotic Symptoms Duration\nmanifestation: \"The ideas about hidden messages in the family and medical papers started about a year and a month ago.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, definitely longer than a month. I’ve tried to place it in time because it bothers me not being sure, and I’d say it started about a year and a month ago now.

At first it wasn’t something I talked about much, because I kept thinking maybe I was just noticing odd things in the papers, or maybe I was mixed up about what had been written before. But that feeling that the family papers and some records were being changed, or that there were messages in how they were arranged… that’s been going on since around then. It hasn’t just been a few weeks.

---------- turn 18 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=gpt-5.4 ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F33.3, R43.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s my eating, I think. It’s gotten kind of turned around on me lately. Some days I’ll realize it’s late in the afternoon and I never ate lunch at all, like it just slipped right out of my head. And then other days I’m standing in the kitchen at the counter, picking at crackers or sweets, not even making a proper meal, just eating them one after another.\n\nThe strange part is it’s not even like I’m enjoying it much. Things don’t taste right to me, or they taste flat, but I still keep nibbling. It feels unlike me, and I guess it worried me enough that I thought I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that foods didn’t taste right?\n\nPatient: I can’t put an exact day on it, honestly. I think I first started noticing it around the same time my eating got so mixed up. I’d be halfway through the day and realize I’d skipped lunch completely, not on purpose, just… it never occurred to me. And then other times I’d be in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets right there at the counter, and I’d think, “Why am I still eating this? It doesn’t even taste good.”\n\nIt was that flat taste that caught my attention. Like the food was there, but it wasn’t really registering the way it should. Not spoiled or anything, just not right. And that’s part of what made me come in, because it felt so unlike my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything obvious like that, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the day has already lost its color before I’ve even gotten dressed. I’ll just lie there for a bit and feel like everything is sort of dimmed down, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s a hard feeling to describe because it’s not one big dramatic thing. It’s more like I open my eyes and there’s this weight sitting there with me, and I think, “Oh… it’s going to be that kind of day again.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel most unlike myself. I used to have my little things in the week, you know? My watercolor class, my quilting circle, book club… I would get my bag ready by the door with whatever I needed, and I’d sort of look forward to getting out and seeing everyone.\n\nLately, though, I’ll still put the bag there sometimes, almost out of habit, and then I just… leave it sitting there. I’ll look at it and think, “I should go,” but there’s no pull to it. It’s not even that I hate the idea exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go. And then the time passes, and I feel bad about it afterward, but not enough to change it the next time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping differently than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed some big clear change like staying up all night or sleeping the whole day away. It’s more that I haven’t been paying close attention to it, honestly.\n\nSome nights feel normal enough, and other nights I’m just sort of aware of lying there and thinking, “Am I sleeping right?” but I couldn’t tell you exactly if it’s really different from usual or if I’m just noticing everything more because I haven’t been feeling quite myself. So… I don’t know. Nothing that stands out to me the way the other things have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more forgetful or having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s been happening with little ordinary things, which is what bothers me. Not big complicated tasks, just simple things around the house.\n\nI’ll sit at the kitchen table and think, “All right, I need to call the pharmacy… and I should open the mail too,” and then I just sort of stare at both of those ideas like they’re too much to sort through. My mind gets foggy, like there’s cotton in it, and I can’t decide what to do first. Then ten minutes go by, or longer, and I haven’t called anyone and I haven’t opened the mail either. I’m just sitting there feeling foolish because it shouldn’t be that hard.\n\nIt’s not every single second, but enough that I’ve noticed it. Simple decisions feel harder than they ought to.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: I’d say… about a year and two months now, give or take. It’s hard to mark the exact day something like that starts, because it wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything had changed. It was more like the color slowly drained out of things, little by little, and then at some point I realized, “Oh… this has been going on for quite a while.”\n\nBut yes, if I’m being as honest and accurate as I can, it’s been most days for about fourteen months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think hopeless is probably the right word, though I don’t always call it that to myself.\n\nMost mornings, when I first wake up, there’s this heavy, low feeling already sitting there, before I’ve even gotten dressed or done anything. It’s like the day has lost its color before it’s even started. I’ll lie there for a bit and think, “Well… here we go again,” not in a dramatic way, just tired inside, like I can’t quite imagine the day turning into anything better.\n\nIt isn’t that I’m crying every morning or saying anything out loud. It’s more quiet than that. Just this weight, this dullness, like everything ahead of me is already gray. So yes… lately, I have been feeling pretty hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually up or energetic for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I get a little more done, or I feel a bit less weighed down for a few hours, but not what I would call unusually up or full of energy for several days in a row.\n\nAt my age, energy sort of comes and goes for ordinary reasons, you know? If I sleep a little better one night, I might tidy the kitchen or answer a few calls, and then I think, “Oh, maybe today won’t be so bad.” But it doesn’t turn into several days of feeling revved up or anything like that. I can’t think of a stretch where I was unusually energetic in a way that stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I have had thoughts like that, though it’s hard to say out loud because I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself. I’m not sitting there working out how I would do it or anything like that.\n\nBut I do catch myself thinking… if I didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be all right. Sometimes it even feels like it might be a relief, not just for me, but for everyone around me. Like I’m tired, and maybe other people are tired of me being this way too.\n\nI know that sounds awful. I don’t mean that I’m planning anything. It’s more like a quiet thought that comes when I’m very low, that maybe just not being here would be easier.\n\nDoctor: During this past year or so, was there ever a stretch of more than two months when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there was a stretch that long. I’ve had little times where things felt a bit quieter, like the worries about the records weren’t pressing on me quite as much, or I could get through a few days without feeling so stirred up about that. But when I really look back over the past year, the sadness has still been there most of the time.\n\nIt started taking over more after my sister became ill, and since then it’s like… even if one worry eased up for a while, I still didn’t feel like myself. I wasn’t really enjoying things, and there was this dullness underneath everything. So, no, I can’t honestly say there were more than two months where I wasn’t depressed. It’s been more like it faded a little here and there, but never really lifted for that long.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year and two months, were you feeling depressed most days for at least another ten months?\n\nPatient: No… not another ten months before that. I don’t think so.\n\nWhen I try to put a shape to it, the really low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months. Before that, I may have had ordinary sad days, or times I felt tired or discouraged, but not depressed most days for that long. It’s this past year or so that feels like it settled in and stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have been worrying a lot. It’s not just ordinary worrying, like about bills or getting to appointments, though there is some of that too. It’s more this feeling that I can’t put down, about the family papers and records.\n\nI know it sounds odd when I say it out loud, and I’m aware people may think I’m making too much of it, but I keep feeling like certain dates and names in the family papers are being changed, or hidden from me somehow. Like I’ll look at something and think, “That wasn’t how it was before,” or I’ll feel like a name has been placed there or left out for a reason. And then my mind starts going over and over it.\n\nSometimes it feels like the hospital, or maybe someone in the family, is sending little messages through the records. Not a message spoken out loud, exactly, but like the way the dates line up, or which names are included, is meant to tell me something. I know that probably sounds strange, but it feels very real when I’m in it, and then I can’t stop thinking about what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that the family papers and records are being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say “for certain” in the way you mean… like if you asked me to prove it, I don’t have proof I could lay out on the table. But it feels very strong to me, stronger than just a passing thought.\n\nI keep feeling that certain dates and names in the family papers have been changed, or maybe kept from me, and then I start wondering why that would be. It’s like there’s a pattern there that I’m supposed to notice. Sometimes I think it’s the hospital, or someone in the family, using the records to send me little messages—not in words exactly, but in what’s included, what’s missing, how things line up.\n\nI know it sounds odd. I do know that. Part of me can hear how it must sound to someone else. But when I’m looking at those papers, it doesn’t feel odd at all—it feels like something is really happening, and I can’t just dismiss it.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about the family papers being changed or used to send you messages ever been present during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… there was a time like that. It’s a little hard to explain, because the low mood sort of comes and goes in heaviness, but there were several weeks when I wasn’t feeling as sunk down as usual.\n\nI was eating more normally then, and I was getting dressed more normally too—not just sitting around in the same things or feeling like everything was too much effort. So in that sense, my mood had eased some.\n\nBut even during those weeks, I still had that strong feeling about the papers. I still felt like the dates and names in the records were being changed, or arranged in a certain way, like they were meant to signal something to me. It didn’t go away just because I was functioning a bit better. If anything, I remember being more able to look at the papers and think about them, and the feeling was still there, that something in them was being altered or used to tell me something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way people usually mean, like hearing a clear voice in the room when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a sound from another room, and then when I stop and listen there isn’t anything. But I don’t know if that’s just the house settling, or my ears, or me being tired. I don’t really have a voice talking to me, telling me things, or carrying on a conversation. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIf you mean like actual words, like someone speaking when no one is there, then no… I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do foods actually taste different to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do, or at least eating feels different than it used to. It’s hard for me to separate “taste” from just not wanting food, if that makes sense.\n\nSome things don’t taste right anymore. Not spoiled exactly, but flat, or strange, or just not like I remember. And then my eating gets all mixed up around that. Some days I’ll realize I’ve forgotten lunch completely, like the whole middle of the day went by and I never even thought to eat. Then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, almost just because they’re there, even though I’m not really enjoying them and they don’t taste especially good.\n\nThat’s actually one of the things that made me come in, because it doesn’t feel like my normal way of eating. It’s gotten turned around.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you had that belief about the family papers and records being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely longer than a month. I’ve tried to place it in time because it bothers me not being sure, and I’d say it started about a year and a month ago now.\n\nAt first it wasn’t something I talked about much, because I kept thinking maybe I was just noticing odd things in the papers, or maybe I was mixed up about what had been written before. But that feeling that the family papers and some records were being changed, or that there were messages in how they were arranged… that’s been going on since around then. It hasn’t just been a few weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

If anything, I remember being more able to look at the papers and think about them, and the feeling was still there, that something in them was being altered or used to tell me something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way people usually mean, like hearing a clear voice in the room when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a sound from another room, and then when I stop and listen there isn’t anything. Then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, almost just because they’re there, even though I’m not really enjoying them and they don’t taste especially good.\n\nThat’s actually one of the things that made me come in, because it doesn’t feel like my normal way of eating. It’s gotten turned around.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you had that belief about the family papers and records being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely longer than a month. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s my eating, I think. It’s gotten kind of turned around on me lately. I’ll look at it and think, “I should go,” but there’s no pull to it. It’s not even that I hate the idea exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go. So… I don’t know. Nothing that stands out to me the way the other things have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more forgetful or having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s been happening with little ordinary things, which is what bothers me. Not big complicated tasks, just simple things around the house.\n\nI’ll sit at the kitchen table and think, “All right, I need to call the pharmacy… and I should open the mail too,” and then I just sort of stare at both of those ideas like they’re too much to sort through. My mind gets foggy, like there’s cotton in it, and I can’t decide what to do first. Then ten minutes go by, or longer, and I haven’t called anyone and I haven’t opened the mail either. I’m just sitting there feeling foolish because it shouldn’t be that hard.\n\nIt’s not every single second, but enough that I’ve noticed it. Simple decisions feel harder than they ought to.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: I’d say… about a year and two months now, give or take. It’s hard to mark the exact day something like that starts, because it wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything had changed. It was more like the color slowly drained out of things, little by little, and then at some point I realized, “Oh… this has been going on for quite a while.”\n\nBut yes, if I’m being as honest and accurate as I can, it’s been most days for about fourteen months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think hopeless is probably the right word, though I don’t always call it that to myself.\n\nMost mornings, when I first wake up, there’s this heavy, low feeling already sitting there, before I’ve even gotten dressed or done anything. It’s like the day has lost its color before it’s even started. I’ll lie there for a bit and think, “Well… here we go again,” not in a dramatic way, just tired inside, like I can’t quite imagine the day turning into anything better.\n\nIt isn’t that I’m crying every morning or saying anything out loud. It’s more quiet than that. Just this weight, this dullness, like everything ahead of me is already gray. So yes… lately, I have been feeling pretty hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually up or energetic for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I get a little more done, or I feel a bit less weighed down for a few hours, but not what I would call unusually up or full of energy for several days in a row.\n\nAt my age, energy sort of comes and goes for ordinary reasons, you know? If I sleep a little better one night, I might tidy the kitchen or answer a few calls, and then I think, “Oh, maybe today won’t be so bad.” But it doesn’t turn into several days of feeling revved up or anything like that. I can’t think of a stretch where I was unusually energetic in a way that stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I have had thoughts like that, though it’s hard to say out loud because I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself. I’m not sitting there working out how I would do it or anything like that.\n\nBut I do catch myself thinking… if I didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be all right. Sometimes it even feels like it might be a relief, not just for me, but for everyone around me. Like I’m tired, and maybe other people are tired of me being this way too.\n\nI know that sounds awful. I don’t mean that I’m planning anything. It’s more like a quiet thought that comes when I’m very low, that maybe just not being here would be easier.\n\nDoctor: During this past year or so, was there ever a stretch of more than two months when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there was a stretch that long. But when I really look back over the past year, the sadness has still been there most of the time.\n\nIt started taking over more after my sister became ill, and since then it’s like… even if one worry eased up for a while, I still didn’t feel like myself. I wasn’t really enjoying things, and there was this dullness underneath everything. So, no, I can’t honestly say there were more than two months where I wasn’t depressed. It’s been more like it faded a little here and there, but never really lifted for that long.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year and two months, were you feeling depressed most days for at least another ten months?\n\nPatient: No… not another ten months before that. I don’t think so.\n\nWhen I try to put a shape to it, the really low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months. Before that, I may have had ordinary sad days, or times I felt tired or discouraged, but not depressed most days for that long. It’s this past year or so that feels like it settled in and stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have been worrying a lot. It’s not just ordinary worrying, like about bills or getting to appointments, though there is some of that too. It’s more this feeling that I can’t put down, about the family papers and records.\n\nI know it sounds odd when I say it out loud, and I’m aware people may think I’m making too much of it, but I keep feeling like certain dates and names in the family papers are being changed, or hidden from me somehow. Like I’ll look at something and think, “That wasn’t how it was before,” or I’ll feel like a name has been placed there or left out for a reason. And then my mind starts going over and over it.\n\nSometimes it feels like the hospital, or maybe someone in the family, is sending little messages through the records. Not a message spoken out loud, exactly, but like the way the dates line up, or which names are included, is meant to tell me something. I know that probably sounds strange, but it feels very real when I’m in it, and then I can’t stop thinking about what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that the family papers and records are being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say “for certain” in the way you mean… like if you asked me to prove it, I don’t have proof I could lay out on the table. But it feels very strong to me, stronger than just a passing thought.\n\nI keep feeling that certain dates and names in the family papers have been changed, or maybe kept from me, and then I start wondering why that would be. It’s like there’s a pattern there that I’m supposed to notice. Sometimes I think it’s the hospital, or someone in the family, using the records to send me little messages—not in words exactly, but in what’s included, what’s missing, how things line up.\n\nI know it sounds odd. I do know that. Part of me can hear how it must sound to someone else. But when I’m looking at those papers, it doesn’t feel odd at all—it feels like something is really happening, and I can’t just dismiss it.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about the family papers being changed or used to send you messages ever been present during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… there was a time like that. It’s a little hard to explain, because the low mood sort of comes and goes in heaviness, but there were several weeks when I wasn’t feeling as sunk down as usual.\n\nI was eating more normally then, and I was getting dressed more normally too—not just sitting around in the same things or feeling like everything was too much effort. So in that sense, my mood had eased some.\n\nBut even during those weeks, I still had that strong feeling about the papers. I still felt like the dates and names in the records were being changed, or arranged in a certain way, like they were meant to signal something to me. It didn’t go away just because I was functioning a bit better. If anything, I remember being more able to look at the papers and think about them, and the feeling was still there, that something in them was being altered or used to tell me something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way people usually mean, like hearing a clear voice in the room when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a sound from another room, and then when I stop and listen there isn’t anything. But I don’t know if that’s just the house settling, or my ears, or me being tired. I don’t really have a voice talking to me, telling me things, or carrying on a conversation. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIf you mean like actual words, like someone speaking when no one is there, then no… I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do foods actually taste different to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do, or at least eating feels different than it used to. It’s hard for me to separate “taste” from just not wanting food, if that makes sense.\n\nSome things don’t taste right anymore. Not spoiled exactly, but flat, or strange, or just not like I remember. And then my eating gets all mixed up around that. Some days I’ll realize I’ve forgotten lunch completely, like the whole middle of the day went by and I never even thought to eat. Then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, almost just because they’re there, even though I’m not really enjoying them and they don’t taste especially good.\n\nThat’s actually one of the things that made me come in, because it doesn’t feel like my normal way of eating. It’s gotten turned around.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you had that belief about the family papers and records being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely longer than a month. I’ve tried to place it in time because it bothers me not being sure, and I’d say it started about a year and a month ago now.\n\nAt first it wasn’t something I talked about much, because I kept thinking maybe I was just noticing odd things in the papers, or maybe I was mixed up about what had been written before. But that feeling that the family papers and some records were being changed, or that there were messages in how they were arranged… that’s been going on since around then. It hasn’t just been a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your sense of smell is different than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, I haven’t noticed smells being stronger or weaker, or smelling things that other people don’t seem to smell.\n\nSometimes I’ll think something smells a little off, like in the kitchen or with food, but then I can’t tell if that’s just the food itself or the room or maybe me paying too much attention to it. But as far as my sense of smell actually changing from how it used to be, I wouldn’t really say that. Nothing stands out to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So… I don’t know. Nothing that stands out to me the way the other things have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more forgetful or having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. My mind gets foggy, like there’s cotton in it, and I can’t decide what to do first. Then ten minutes go by, or longer, and I haven’t called anyone and I haven’t opened the mail either. It’s like the day has lost its color before it’s even started. I’ll lie there for a bit and think, “Well… here we go again,” not in a dramatic way, just tired inside, like I can’t quite imagine the day turning into anything better.\n\nIt isn’t that I’m crying every morning or saying anything out loud. It’s more quiet than that. Just this weight, this dullness, like everything ahead of me is already gray. So yes… lately, I have been feeling pretty hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually up or energetic for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I get a little more done, or I feel a bit less weighed down for a few hours, but not what I would call unusually up or full of energy for several days in a row.\n\nAt my age, energy sort of comes and goes for ordinary reasons, you know? If I sleep a little better one night, I might tidy the kitchen or answer a few calls, and then I think, “Oh, maybe today won’t be so bad.” But it doesn’t turn into several days of feeling revved up or anything like that. I can’t think of a stretch where I was unusually energetic in a way that stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I have had thoughts like that, though it’s hard to say out loud because I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself. I’m not sitting there working out how I would do it or anything like that.\n\nBut I do catch myself thinking… if I didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be all right. Sometimes it even feels like it might be a relief, not just for me, but for everyone around me. Like I’m tired, and maybe other people are tired of me being this way too.\n\nI know that sounds awful. I don’t mean that I’m planning anything. It’s more like a quiet thought that comes when I’m very low, that maybe just not being here would be easier.\n\nDoctor: During this past year or so, was there ever a stretch of more than two months when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there was a stretch that long. I’ve had little times where things felt a bit quieter, like the worries about the records weren’t pressing on me quite as much, or I could get through a few days without feeling so stirred up about that. I wasn’t really enjoying things, and there was this dullness underneath everything. So, no, I can’t honestly say there were more than two months where I wasn’t depressed. It hasn’t just been a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your sense of smell is different than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, I haven’t noticed smells being stronger or weaker, or smelling things that other people don’t seem to smell.\n\nSometimes I’ll think something smells a little off, like in the kitchen or with food, but then I can’t tell if that’s just the food itself or the room or maybe me paying too much attention to it. But as far as my sense of smell actually changing from how it used to be, I wouldn’t really say that. Nothing stands out to me.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel that anyone can put thoughts into your mind?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 74 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds odd, but I keep feeling that certain dates and names in our family papers are being changed or hidden from me, like the hospital or someone in the family is sending me little messages through the records.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say someone is putting thoughts directly into my mind, like planting them there word for word. That sounds a little stronger than how I experience it.

But I do keep feeling like certain things are being pointed at me, if that makes sense. Like with the family papers and records… dates, names, little details seem changed or hidden, and then I feel like there’s a message in it. Sometimes I think maybe the hospital, or maybe someone in the family, is trying to get something across to me through how the records look or what’s missing. I know that probably sounds odd, and I’m not saying I hear it like a voice in my head, but the thought comes up very strongly that it’s meant for me, like I’m supposed to notice it.

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n20\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F33.3, R43.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… mostly it’s my eating, I think. It’s gotten kind of turned around on me lately. Some days I’ll realize it’s late in the afternoon and I never ate lunch at all, like it just slipped right out of my head. And then other days I’m standing in the kitchen at the counter, picking at crackers or sweets, not even making a proper meal, just eating them one after another.\n\nThe strange part is it’s not even like I’m enjoying it much. Things don’t taste right to me, or they taste flat, but I still keep nibbling. It feels unlike me, and I guess it worried me enough that I thought I should come in and talk to someone.\n\nDoctor: When did you first notice that foods didn’t taste right?\n\nPatient: I can’t put an exact day on it, honestly. I think I first started noticing it around the same time my eating got so mixed up. I’d be halfway through the day and realize I’d skipped lunch completely, not on purpose, just… it never occurred to me. And then other times I’d be in the kitchen eating crackers or sweets right there at the counter, and I’d think, “Why am I still eating this? It doesn’t even taste good.”\n\nIt was that flat taste that caught my attention. Like the food was there, but it wasn’t really registering the way it should. Not spoiled or anything, just not right. And that’s part of what made me come in, because it felt so unlike my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have. It’s not always like I’m crying or anything obvious like that, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the day has already lost its color before I’ve even gotten dressed. I’ll just lie there for a bit and feel like everything is sort of dimmed down, if that makes sense.\n\nIt’s a hard feeling to describe because it’s not one big dramatic thing. It’s more like I open my eyes and there’s this weight sitting there with me, and I think, “Oh… it’s going to be that kind of day again.”\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than usual lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel most unlike myself. I used to have my little things in the week, you know? My watercolor class, my quilting circle, book club… I would get my bag ready by the door with whatever I needed, and I’d sort of look forward to getting out and seeing everyone.\n\nLately, though, I’ll still put the bag there sometimes, almost out of habit, and then I just… leave it sitting there. I’ll look at it and think, “I should go,” but there’s no pull to it. It’s not even that I hate the idea exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough to get up and go. And then the time passes, and I feel bad about it afterward, but not enough to change it the next time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been sleeping differently than usual lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed some big clear change like staying up all night or sleeping the whole day away. It’s more that I haven’t been paying close attention to it, honestly.\n\nSome nights feel normal enough, and other nights I’m just sort of aware of lying there and thinking, “Am I sleeping right?” but I couldn’t tell you exactly if it’s really different from usual or if I’m just noticing everything more because I haven’t been feeling quite myself. So… I don’t know. Nothing that stands out to me the way the other things have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been more forgetful or having trouble concentrating lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. It’s been happening with little ordinary things, which is what bothers me. Not big complicated tasks, just simple things around the house.\n\nI’ll sit at the kitchen table and think, “All right, I need to call the pharmacy… and I should open the mail too,” and then I just sort of stare at both of those ideas like they’re too much to sort through. My mind gets foggy, like there’s cotton in it, and I can’t decide what to do first. Then ten minutes go by, or longer, and I haven’t called anyone and I haven’t opened the mail either. I’m just sitting there feeling foolish because it shouldn’t be that hard.\n\nIt’s not every single second, but enough that I’ve noticed it. Simple decisions feel harder than they ought to.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this low most days?\n\nPatient: I’d say… about a year and two months now, give or take. It’s hard to mark the exact day something like that starts, because it wasn’t like I woke up one morning and everything had changed. It was more like the color slowly drained out of things, little by little, and then at some point I realized, “Oh… this has been going on for quite a while.”\n\nBut yes, if I’m being as honest and accurate as I can, it’s been most days for about fourteen months.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think hopeless is probably the right word, though I don’t always call it that to myself.\n\nMost mornings, when I first wake up, there’s this heavy, low feeling already sitting there, before I’ve even gotten dressed or done anything. It’s like the day has lost its color before it’s even started. I’ll lie there for a bit and think, “Well… here we go again,” not in a dramatic way, just tired inside, like I can’t quite imagine the day turning into anything better.\n\nIt isn’t that I’m crying every morning or saying anything out loud. It’s more quiet than that. Just this weight, this dullness, like everything ahead of me is already gray. So yes… lately, I have been feeling pretty hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually up or energetic for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say that, no. I mean… I’ve had days where I get a little more done, or I feel a bit less weighed down for a few hours, but not what I would call unusually up or full of energy for several days in a row.\n\nAt my age, energy sort of comes and goes for ordinary reasons, you know? If I sleep a little better one night, I might tidy the kitchen or answer a few calls, and then I think, “Oh, maybe today won’t be so bad.” But it doesn’t turn into several days of feeling revved up or anything like that. I can’t think of a stretch where I was unusually energetic in a way that stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: I… yes, I have had thoughts like that, though it’s hard to say out loud because I don’t want anyone to think I’m about to do something. I don’t have a plan to hurt myself. I’m not sitting there working out how I would do it or anything like that.\n\nBut I do catch myself thinking… if I didn’t wake up one morning, maybe that would be all right. Sometimes it even feels like it might be a relief, not just for me, but for everyone around me. Like I’m tired, and maybe other people are tired of me being this way too.\n\nI know that sounds awful. I don’t mean that I’m planning anything. It’s more like a quiet thought that comes when I’m very low, that maybe just not being here would be easier.\n\nDoctor: During this past year or so, was there ever a stretch of more than two months when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there was a stretch that long. I’ve had little times where things felt a bit quieter, like the worries about the records weren’t pressing on me quite as much, or I could get through a few days without feeling so stirred up about that. But when I really look back over the past year, the sadness has still been there most of the time.\n\nIt started taking over more after my sister became ill, and since then it’s like… even if one worry eased up for a while, I still didn’t feel like myself. I wasn’t really enjoying things, and there was this dullness underneath everything. So, no, I can’t honestly say there were more than two months where I wasn’t depressed. It’s been more like it faded a little here and there, but never really lifted for that long.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year and two months, were you feeling depressed most days for at least another ten months?\n\nPatient: No… not another ten months before that. I don’t think so.\n\nWhen I try to put a shape to it, the really low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months. Before that, I may have had ordinary sad days, or times I felt tired or discouraged, but not depressed most days for that long. It’s this past year or so that feels like it settled in and stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have been worrying a lot. It’s not just ordinary worrying, like about bills or getting to appointments, though there is some of that too. It’s more this feeling that I can’t put down, about the family papers and records.\n\nI know it sounds odd when I say it out loud, and I’m aware people may think I’m making too much of it, but I keep feeling like certain dates and names in the family papers are being changed, or hidden from me somehow. Like I’ll look at something and think, “That wasn’t how it was before,” or I’ll feel like a name has been placed there or left out for a reason. And then my mind starts going over and over it.\n\nSometimes it feels like the hospital, or maybe someone in the family, is sending little messages through the records. Not a message spoken out loud, exactly, but like the way the dates line up, or which names are included, is meant to tell me something. I know that probably sounds strange, but it feels very real when I’m in it, and then I can’t stop thinking about what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that the family papers and records are being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say “for certain” in the way you mean… like if you asked me to prove it, I don’t have proof I could lay out on the table. But it feels very strong to me, stronger than just a passing thought.\n\nI keep feeling that certain dates and names in the family papers have been changed, or maybe kept from me, and then I start wondering why that would be. It’s like there’s a pattern there that I’m supposed to notice. Sometimes I think it’s the hospital, or someone in the family, using the records to send me little messages—not in words exactly, but in what’s included, what’s missing, how things line up.\n\nI know it sounds odd. I do know that. Part of me can hear how it must sound to someone else. But when I’m looking at those papers, it doesn’t feel odd at all—it feels like something is really happening, and I can’t just dismiss it.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about the family papers being changed or used to send you messages ever been present during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… there was a time like that. It’s a little hard to explain, because the low mood sort of comes and goes in heaviness, but there were several weeks when I wasn’t feeling as sunk down as usual.\n\nI was eating more normally then, and I was getting dressed more normally too—not just sitting around in the same things or feeling like everything was too much effort. So in that sense, my mood had eased some.\n\nBut even during those weeks, I still had that strong feeling about the papers. I still felt like the dates and names in the records were being changed, or arranged in a certain way, like they were meant to signal something to me. It didn’t go away just because I was functioning a bit better. If anything, I remember being more able to look at the papers and think about them, and the feeling was still there, that something in them was being altered or used to tell me something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way people usually mean, like hearing a clear voice in the room when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a sound from another room, and then when I stop and listen there isn’t anything. But I don’t know if that’s just the house settling, or my ears, or me being tired. I don’t really have a voice talking to me, telling me things, or carrying on a conversation. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIf you mean like actual words, like someone speaking when no one is there, then no… I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do foods actually taste different to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do, or at least eating feels different than it used to. It’s hard for me to separate “taste” from just not wanting food, if that makes sense.\n\nSome things don’t taste right anymore. Not spoiled exactly, but flat, or strange, or just not like I remember. And then my eating gets all mixed up around that. Some days I’ll realize I’ve forgotten lunch completely, like the whole middle of the day went by and I never even thought to eat. Then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, almost just because they’re there, even though I’m not really enjoying them and they don’t taste especially good.\n\nThat’s actually one of the things that made me come in, because it doesn’t feel like my normal way of eating. It’s gotten turned around.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you had that belief about the family papers and records being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely longer than a month. I’ve tried to place it in time because it bothers me not being sure, and I’d say it started about a year and a month ago now.\n\nAt first it wasn’t something I talked about much, because I kept thinking maybe I was just noticing odd things in the papers, or maybe I was mixed up about what had been written before. But that feeling that the family papers and some records were being changed, or that there were messages in how they were arranged… that’s been going on since around then. It hasn’t just been a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your sense of smell is different than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, I haven’t noticed smells being stronger or weaker, or smelling things that other people don’t seem to smell.\n\nSometimes I’ll think something smells a little off, like in the kitchen or with food, but then I can’t tell if that’s just the food itself or the room or maybe me paying too much attention to it. But as far as my sense of smell actually changing from how it used to be, I wouldn’t really say that. Nothing stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that anyone can put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say someone is putting thoughts directly into my mind, like planting them there word for word. That sounds a little stronger than how I experience it.\n\nBut I do keep feeling like certain things are being pointed at me, if that makes sense. Like with the family papers and records… dates, names, little details seem changed or hidden, and then I feel like there’s a message in it. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It’s more like a quiet thought that comes when I’m very low, that maybe just not being here would be easier.\n\nDoctor: During this past year or so, was there ever a stretch of more than two months when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think there was a stretch that long. I’ve had little times where things felt a bit quieter, like the worries about the records weren’t pressing on me quite as much, or I could get through a few days without feeling so stirred up about that. But when I really look back over the past year, the sadness has still been there most of the time.\n\nIt started taking over more after my sister became ill, and since then it’s like… even if one worry eased up for a while, I still didn’t feel like myself. I wasn’t really enjoying things, and there was this dullness underneath everything. So, no, I can’t honestly say there were more than two months where I wasn’t depressed. It’s been more like it faded a little here and there, but never really lifted for that long.\n\nDoctor: Before this past year and two months, were you feeling depressed most days for at least another ten months?\n\nPatient: No… not another ten months before that. I don’t think so.\n\nWhen I try to put a shape to it, the really low, colorless stretch has been with me for about a year and two months. Before that, I may have had ordinary sad days, or times I felt tired or discouraged, but not depressed most days for that long. It’s this past year or so that feels like it settled in and stayed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been worrying a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have been worrying a lot. It’s not just ordinary worrying, like about bills or getting to appointments, though there is some of that too. It’s more this feeling that I can’t put down, about the family papers and records.\n\nI know it sounds odd when I say it out loud, and I’m aware people may think I’m making too much of it, but I keep feeling like certain dates and names in the family papers are being changed, or hidden from me somehow. Like I’ll look at something and think, “That wasn’t how it was before,” or I’ll feel like a name has been placed there or left out for a reason. And then my mind starts going over and over it.\n\nSometimes it feels like the hospital, or maybe someone in the family, is sending little messages through the records. Not a message spoken out loud, exactly, but like the way the dates line up, or which names are included, is meant to tell me something. I know that probably sounds strange, but it feels very real when I’m in it, and then I can’t stop thinking about what it means.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe for certain that the family papers and records are being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say “for certain” in the way you mean… like if you asked me to prove it, I don’t have proof I could lay out on the table. But it feels very strong to me, stronger than just a passing thought.\n\nI keep feeling that certain dates and names in the family papers have been changed, or maybe kept from me, and then I start wondering why that would be. It’s like there’s a pattern there that I’m supposed to notice. Sometimes I think it’s the hospital, or someone in the family, using the records to send me little messages—not in words exactly, but in what’s included, what’s missing, how things line up.\n\nI know it sounds odd. I do know that. Part of me can hear how it must sound to someone else. But when I’m looking at those papers, it doesn’t feel odd at all—it feels like something is really happening, and I can’t just dismiss it.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs about the family papers being changed or used to send you messages ever been present during a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes… there was a time like that. It’s a little hard to explain, because the low mood sort of comes and goes in heaviness, but there were several weeks when I wasn’t feeling as sunk down as usual.\n\nI was eating more normally then, and I was getting dressed more normally too—not just sitting around in the same things or feeling like everything was too much effort. So in that sense, my mood had eased some.\n\nBut even during those weeks, I still had that strong feeling about the papers. I still felt like the dates and names in the records were being changed, or arranged in a certain way, like they were meant to signal something to me. It didn’t go away just because I was functioning a bit better. If anything, I remember being more able to look at the papers and think about them, and the feeling was still there, that something in them was being altered or used to tell me something.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one is there?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, not in the way people usually mean, like hearing a clear voice in the room when nobody is there.\n\nSometimes I’ll think I heard something, like a sound from another room, and then when I stop and listen there isn’t anything. But I don’t know if that’s just the house settling, or my ears, or me being tired. I don’t really have a voice talking to me, telling me things, or carrying on a conversation. At least, I haven’t noticed that.\n\nIf you mean like actual words, like someone speaking when no one is there, then no… I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do foods actually taste different to you than they used to?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think they do, or at least eating feels different than it used to. It’s hard for me to separate “taste” from just not wanting food, if that makes sense.\n\nSome things don’t taste right anymore. Not spoiled exactly, but flat, or strange, or just not like I remember. And then my eating gets all mixed up around that. Some days I’ll realize I’ve forgotten lunch completely, like the whole middle of the day went by and I never even thought to eat. Then other days I’m standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, almost just because they’re there, even though I’m not really enjoying them and they don’t taste especially good.\n\nThat’s actually one of the things that made me come in, because it doesn’t feel like my normal way of eating. It’s gotten turned around.\n\nDoctor: For at least one month, have you had that belief about the family papers and records being changed or used to send you messages?\n\nPatient: Yes, definitely longer than a month. I’ve tried to place it in time because it bothers me not being sure, and I’d say it started about a year and a month ago now.\n\nAt first it wasn’t something I talked about much, because I kept thinking maybe I was just noticing odd things in the papers, or maybe I was mixed up about what had been written before. But that feeling that the family papers and some records were being changed, or that there were messages in how they were arranged… that’s been going on since around then. It hasn’t just been a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your sense of smell is different than it used to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in any clear way. I mean, I haven’t noticed smells being stronger or weaker, or smelling things that other people don’t seem to smell.\n\nSometimes I’ll think something smells a little off, like in the kitchen or with food, but then I can’t tell if that’s just the food itself or the room or maybe me paying too much attention to it. But as far as my sense of smell actually changing from how it used to be, I wouldn’t really say that. Nothing stands out to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that anyone can put thoughts into your mind?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say someone is putting thoughts directly into my mind, like planting them there word for word. That sounds a little stronger than how I experience it.\n\nBut I do keep feeling like certain things are being pointed at me, if that makes sense. Like with the family papers and records… dates, names, little details seem changed or hidden, and then I feel like there’s a message in it. Sometimes I think maybe the hospital, or maybe someone in the family, is trying to get something across to me through how the records look or what’s missing. I know that probably sounds odd, and I’m not saying I hear it like a voice in my head, but the thought comes up very strongly that it’s meant for me, like I’m supposed to notice it."
  }
